People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Um… I guess I’ve just been feeling kind of watched at work. I drive routes, and lately it seems like dispatch and compliance are tracking when I change things in the computer system, and then there’ll be little comments over the radio that feel… pointed.

I know that might sound like normal work oversight, but it just feels more targeted than that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that these comments and actions by dispatch, compliance, and other drivers are actually about you, specifically?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to jump to conclusions, because I know workplaces monitor things and maybe some of it is normal. But it does feel like it’s about me specifically.\n\nLike when I change something in the route system, then there’ll be a comment over the radio that sounds connected to that, and it feels pointed. I’d like to think it would stop if I wasn’t driving routes anymore… I mean, maybe a different job wouldn’t have the same radio stuff or the route system, so maybe it wouldn’t feel as obvious.

I’m not trying to make it sound bigger than it is. That’s just how it feels.

I know that probably sounds bad when I say it out loud, but… that’s what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these feelings of being watched and targeted would still happen if you changed jobs or weren't driving routes anymore?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think it would stop if I wasn’t driving routes anymore… I mean, maybe a different job wouldn’t have the same radio stuff or the route system, so maybe it wouldn’t feel as obvious.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t know. The main thing is, with this job, dispatch and compliance can track changes I make in the computer, and then those little comments come over the radio, and certain drivers seem like they’re reporting on me. So if I changed jobs, maybe that exact setup wouldn’t be there… but I guess I worry it might still happen in some form.\n\nI’m not trying to make it sound bigger than it is. That’s just how it feels.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think these people, like dispatch and the other drivers, are actually thinking about you when you're not at work?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 29 / transportation_worker / associates\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I’m here because dispatch and compliance have been tracking my route changes through the computer system and little comments on the radio, and even when people say it’s just normal oversight, I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. I don’t know for sure what they’re thinking when I’m not there… I don’t want to sound like I think everyone’s sitting around focused on me all day.

That’s just how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people, like dispatch and the other drivers, are actually thinking about you when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t know for sure what they’re thinking when I’m not there… I don’t want to sound like I think everyone’s sitting around focused on me all day.\n\nBut, yeah, sometimes it feels like it carries over. Like if dispatch or compliance can see my route changes in the computer, and then I hear those little radio comments later, it makes me think they’ve been talking about it, or that certain drivers are reporting things even when I’m not right there.\n\nI know that might sound like I’m reading into it too much… I’m just trying to explain it honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't feel like you're being watched or targeted?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah. Like if I’m home and not checking anything from work, I can kind of put it out of my mind for a while.\n\nBut when I’m actually at work, or thinking about the routes, it comes back pretty quickly. Especially if I’ve made a change in the computer and then there’s some little comment on the radio that sounds related… then it feels like, okay, they did see that, and certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nI know there may be normal oversight, and I’m not trying to make it sound dramatic. It’s just hard to not feel watched when it lines up like that. Is that… does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that people are talking about you behind your back when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, sometimes I do think that. Not like everyone is gossiping about me all the time, I don’t want to make it sound like that.\n\nBut when I’m not at work, I still wonder if dispatch or compliance is looking at the route changes I made in the computer and talking about it, or if certain drivers are reporting things about me. Then later, if there’s a little comment on the radio that seems connected, it feels like… okay, that wasn’t just in my head.\n\nI know it could be normal oversight, and I’m trying to be fair about that. But it does feel like they’re talking about me behind my back sometimes. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I drive routes, and lately it seems like dispatch and compliance are tracking when I change things in the computer system, and then there’ll be little comments over the radio that feel… pointed.\n\nI know people say that’s just normal oversight, and maybe I’m not explaining it right, but it feels like certain drivers are reporting on me too. I just thought I should talk to someone before it gets worse, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people, like dispatch and the other drivers, are actually out to harm you or interfere with your life in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “harm” exactly… I don’t want to sound dramatic. I don’t know if “escape” is the right word… I mean, I don’t think I can really stop dispatch or compliance from seeing the changes in the computer. That’s kind of part of the system, I guess.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’ve just been feeling kind of watched at work. I drive routes, and lately it seems like dispatch and compliance are tracking when I change things in the computer system, and then there’ll be little comments over the radio that feel… pointed.\n\nI know people say that’s just normal oversight, and maybe I’m not explaining it right, but it feels like certain drivers are reporting on me too. I just thought I should talk to someone before it gets worse, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people, like dispatch and the other drivers, are actually out to harm you or interfere with your life in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “harm” exactly… I don’t want to sound dramatic. It’s more like interfere, I guess. Like dispatch and compliance can see when I change something in the system, and then these little comments come over the radio that feel aimed at me. And with certain drivers, it really seems like they’re reporting back on what I’m doing.\n\nI know that probably sounds like I’m reading into things, but it feels too connected to be nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this is all part of a larger plan or scheme that's been set up against you?\n\nPatient: I mean… “larger plan” sounds kind of big, and I don’t want to overstate it. But it does feel coordinated sometimes. Like dispatch and compliance can see when I change my route in the system, and then right after, there’ll be little radio comments that feel like they’re about me.\n\nAnd certain drivers… it feels like they’re reporting on me, or at least watching what I’m doing and passing it along. I know that might sound like normal work oversight, but it just feels more targeted than that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that these comments and actions by dispatch, compliance, and other drivers are actually about you, specifically?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to jump to conclusions, because I know workplaces monitor things and maybe some of it is normal. But it does feel like it’s about me specifically.\n\nLike when I change something in the route system, then there’ll be a comment over the radio that sounds connected to that, and it feels pointed. And with a couple of the other drivers, I get the sense they’re watching what I’m doing and reporting it back. I know that probably sounds bad when I say it out loud, but… that’s what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these feelings of being watched and targeted would still happen if you changed jobs or weren't driving routes anymore?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think it would stop if I wasn’t driving routes anymore… I mean, maybe a different job wouldn’t have the same radio stuff or the route system, so maybe it wouldn’t feel as obvious.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t know. The main thing is, with this job, dispatch and compliance can track changes I make in the computer, and then those little comments come over the radio, and certain drivers seem like they’re reporting on me. So if I changed jobs, maybe that exact setup wouldn’t be there… but I guess I worry it might still happen in some form.\n\nI’m not trying to make it sound bigger than it is. That’s just how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people, like dispatch and the other drivers, are actually thinking about you when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t know for sure what they’re thinking when I’m not there… I don’t want to sound like I think everyone’s sitting around focused on me all day.\n\nBut, yeah, sometimes it feels like it carries over. Like if dispatch or compliance can see my route changes in the computer, and then I hear those little radio comments later, it makes me think they’ve been talking about it, or that certain drivers are reporting things even when I’m not right there.\n\nI know that might sound like I’m reading into it too much… I’m just trying to explain it honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't feel like you're being watched or targeted?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah. Like if I’m home and not checking anything from work, I can kind of put it out of my mind for a while.\n\nBut when I’m actually at work, or thinking about the routes, it comes back pretty quickly. Especially if I’ve made a change in the computer and then there’s some little comment on the radio that sounds related… then it feels like, okay, they did see that, and certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nI know there may be normal oversight, and I’m not trying to make it sound dramatic. It’s just hard to not feel watched when it lines up like that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't feel like you were being watched or targeted for at least a few weeks?\n\nPatient: A few weeks… I’m not sure. I don’t want to exaggerate it, because there are definitely days when it’s not right in my face, especially if I’m off work and staying away from the work stuff.\n\nBut for a few weeks straight? I don’t think so, not really. It kind of stays in the back of my mind that dispatch or compliance can see the route changes in the computer, and then if I hear one of those little radio comments when I’m back, it brings it all right back. And I start thinking certain drivers have been reporting on me again.\n\nI know that may sound like I’m making too much of it… but no, I don’t think I’ve gone a few weeks where I didn’t feel that at least a little.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any way to stop or escape the people you feel are watching or targeting you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “escape” is the right word… I mean, I don’t think I can really stop dispatch or compliance from seeing the changes in the computer. That’s kind of part of the system, I guess.\n\nI try to keep things normal and not make extra changes, and sometimes I’ll avoid thinking about it, but if the radio comments start sounding like they’re about what I did, then it feels like certain drivers are still reporting on me anyway.\n\nSo… I don’t really know how to stop it. I just try not to give them anything to focus on, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the people you think are watching or targeting you have any special powers or abilities that allow them to do so?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it’s like special powers or anything like that. I just thought I should talk to someone before it gets worse, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people, like dispatch and the other drivers, are actually out to harm you or interfere with your life in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “harm” exactly… I don’t want to sound dramatic. I drive routes, and lately it seems like dispatch and compliance are tracking when I change things in the computer system, and then there’ll be little comments over the radio that feel… pointed.\n\nI know people say that’s just normal oversight, and maybe I’m not explaining it right, but it feels like certain drivers are reporting on me too. I just thought I should talk to someone before it gets worse, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people, like dispatch and the other drivers, are actually out to harm you or interfere with your life in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “harm” exactly… I don’t want to sound dramatic. It’s more like interfere, I guess. Like dispatch and compliance can see when I change something in the system, and then these little comments come over the radio that feel aimed at me. And with certain drivers, it really seems like they’re reporting back on what I’m doing.\n\nI know that probably sounds like I’m reading into things, but it feels too connected to be nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this is all part of a larger plan or scheme that's been set up against you?\n\nPatient: I mean… “larger plan” sounds kind of big, and I don’t want to overstate it. But it does feel coordinated sometimes. Like dispatch and compliance can see when I change my route in the system, and then right after, there’ll be little radio comments that feel like they’re about me.\n\nAnd certain drivers… it feels like they’re reporting on me, or at least watching what I’m doing and passing it along. I know that might sound like normal work oversight, but it just feels more targeted than that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that these comments and actions by dispatch, compliance, and other drivers are actually about you, specifically?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to jump to conclusions, because I know workplaces monitor things and maybe some of it is normal. But it does feel like it’s about me specifically.\n\nLike when I change something in the route system, then there’ll be a comment over the radio that sounds connected to that, and it feels pointed. And with a couple of the other drivers, I get the sense they’re watching what I’m doing and reporting it back. I know that probably sounds bad when I say it out loud, but… that’s what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these feelings of being watched and targeted would still happen if you changed jobs or weren't driving routes anymore?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think it would stop if I wasn’t driving routes anymore… I mean, maybe a different job wouldn’t have the same radio stuff or the route system, so maybe it wouldn’t feel as obvious.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t know. The main thing is, with this job, dispatch and compliance can track changes I make in the computer, and then those little comments come over the radio, and certain drivers seem like they’re reporting on me. So if I changed jobs, maybe that exact setup wouldn’t be there… but I guess I worry it might still happen in some form.\n\nI’m not trying to make it sound bigger than it is. That’s just how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people, like dispatch and the other drivers, are actually thinking about you when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t know for sure what they’re thinking when I’m not there… I don’t want to sound like I think everyone’s sitting around focused on me all day.\n\nBut, yeah, sometimes it feels like it carries over. Like if dispatch or compliance can see my route changes in the computer, and then I hear those little radio comments later, it makes me think they’ve been talking about it, or that certain drivers are reporting things even when I’m not right there.\n\nI know that might sound like I’m reading into it too much… I’m just trying to explain it honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't feel like you're being watched or targeted?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah. Like if I’m home and not checking anything from work, I can kind of put it out of my mind for a while.\n\nBut when I’m actually at work, or thinking about the routes, it comes back pretty quickly. Especially if I’ve made a change in the computer and then there’s some little comment on the radio that sounds related… then it feels like, okay, they did see that, and certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nI know there may be normal oversight, and I’m not trying to make it sound dramatic. It’s just hard to not feel watched when it lines up like that. Is that… does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that people are talking about you behind your back when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, sometimes I do think that. Not like everyone is gossiping about me all the time, I don’t want to make it sound like that.\n\nBut when I’m not at work, I still wonder if dispatch or compliance is looking at the route changes I made in the computer and talking about it, or if certain drivers are reporting things about me. Then later, if there’s a little comment on the radio that seems connected, it feels like… okay, that wasn’t just in my head.\n\nI know it could be normal oversight, and I’m trying to be fair about that. But it does feel like they’re talking about me behind my back sometimes. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't feel like you were being watched or targeted for at least a few weeks?\n\nPatient: A few weeks… I’m not sure. I don’t want to exaggerate it, because there are definitely days when it’s not right in my face, especially if I’m off work and staying away from the work stuff.\n\nBut for a few weeks straight? I don’t think so, not really. It kind of stays in the back of my mind that dispatch or compliance can see the route changes in the computer, and then if I hear one of those little radio comments when I’m back, it brings it all right back. And I start thinking certain drivers have been reporting on me again.\n\nI know that may sound like I’m making too much of it… but no, I don’t think I’ve gone a few weeks where I didn’t feel that at least a little.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any way to stop or escape the people you feel are watching or targeting you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “escape” is the right word… I mean, I don’t think I can really stop dispatch or compliance from seeing the changes in the computer. I just try not to give them anything to focus on, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the people you think are watching or targeting you have any special powers or abilities that allow them to do so?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it’s like special powers or anything like that.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F22, F60.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’ve just been feeling kind of watched at work. I drive routes, and lately it seems like dispatch and compliance are tracking when I change things in the computer system, and then there’ll be little comments over the radio that feel… pointed.\n\nI know people say that’s just normal oversight, and maybe I’m not explaining it right, but it feels like certain drivers are reporting on me too. I just thought I should talk to someone before it gets worse, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people, like dispatch and the other drivers, are actually out to harm you or interfere with your life in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “harm” exactly… I don’t want to sound dramatic. It’s more like interfere, I guess. Like dispatch and compliance can see when I change something in the system, and then these little comments come over the radio that feel aimed at me. And with certain drivers, it really seems like they’re reporting back on what I’m doing.\n\nI know that probably sounds like I’m reading into things, but it feels too connected to be nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this is all part of a larger plan or scheme that's been set up against you?\n\nPatient: I mean… “larger plan” sounds kind of big, and I don’t want to overstate it. But it does feel coordinated sometimes. Like dispatch and compliance can see when I change my route in the system, and then right after, there’ll be little radio comments that feel like they’re about me.\n\nAnd certain drivers… it feels like they’re reporting on me, or at least watching what I’m doing and passing it along. I know that might sound like normal work oversight, but it just feels more targeted than that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that these comments and actions by dispatch, compliance, and other drivers are actually about you, specifically?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to jump to conclusions, because I know workplaces monitor things and maybe some of it is normal. But it does feel like it’s about me specifically.\n\nLike when I change something in the route system, then there’ll be a comment over the radio that sounds connected to that, and it feels pointed. And with a couple of the other drivers, I get the sense they’re watching what I’m doing and reporting it back. I know that probably sounds bad when I say it out loud, but… that’s what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these feelings of being watched and targeted would still happen if you changed jobs or weren't driving routes anymore?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think it would stop if I wasn’t driving routes anymore… I mean, maybe a different job wouldn’t have the same radio stuff or the route system, so maybe it wouldn’t feel as obvious.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t know. The main thing is, with this job, dispatch and compliance can track changes I make in the computer, and then those little comments come over the radio, and certain drivers seem like they’re reporting on me. So if I changed jobs, maybe that exact setup wouldn’t be there… but I guess I worry it might still happen in some form.\n\nI’m not trying to make it sound bigger than it is. That’s just how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people, like dispatch and the other drivers, are actually thinking about you when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t know for sure what they’re thinking when I’m not there… I don’t want to sound like I think everyone’s sitting around focused on me all day.\n\nBut, yeah, sometimes it feels like it carries over. Like if dispatch or compliance can see my route changes in the computer, and then I hear those little radio comments later, it makes me think they’ve been talking about it, or that certain drivers are reporting things even when I’m not right there.\n\nI know that might sound like I’m reading into it too much… I’m just trying to explain it honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't feel like you're being watched or targeted?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah. Like if I’m home and not checking anything from work, I can kind of put it out of my mind for a while.\n\nBut when I’m actually at work, or thinking about the routes, it comes back pretty quickly. Especially if I’ve made a change in the computer and then there’s some little comment on the radio that sounds related… then it feels like, okay, they did see that, and certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nI know there may be normal oversight, and I’m not trying to make it sound dramatic. It’s just hard to not feel watched when it lines up like that. Is that… does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that people are talking about you behind your back when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, sometimes I do think that. Not like everyone is gossiping about me all the time, I don’t want to make it sound like that.\n\nBut when I’m not at work, I still wonder if dispatch or compliance is looking at the route changes I made in the computer and talking about it, or if certain drivers are reporting things about me. Then later, if there’s a little comment on the radio that seems connected, it feels like… okay, that wasn’t just in my head.\n\nI know it could be normal oversight, and I’m trying to be fair about that. But it does feel like they’re talking about me behind my back sometimes. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't feel like you were being watched or targeted for at least a few weeks?\n\nPatient: A few weeks… I’m not sure. I don’t want to exaggerate it, because there are definitely days when it’s not right in my face, especially if I’m off work and staying away from the work stuff.\n\nBut for a few weeks straight? I don’t think so, not really. It kind of stays in the back of my mind that dispatch or compliance can see the route changes in the computer, and then if I hear one of those little radio comments when I’m back, it brings it all right back. And I start thinking certain drivers have been reporting on me again.\n\nI know that may sound like I’m making too much of it… but no, I don’t think I’ve gone a few weeks where I didn’t feel that at least a little.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any way to stop or escape the people you feel are watching or targeting you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “escape” is the right word… I mean, I don’t think I can really stop dispatch or compliance from seeing the changes in the computer. That’s kind of part of the system, I guess.\n\nI try to keep things normal and not make extra changes, and sometimes I’ll avoid thinking about it, but if the radio comments start sounding like they’re about what I did, then it feels like certain drivers are still reporting on me anyway.\n\nSo… I don’t really know how to stop it. I just try not to give them anything to focus on, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the people you think are watching or targeting you have any special powers or abilities that allow them to do so?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it’s like special powers or anything like that. I’m not saying they can read my mind or do something impossible.\n\nIt’s more… they have access to the computer system, like dispatch and compliance can see the route changes I make, and then it feels like certain drivers report on me or say little things over the radio that connect back to it. So it’s not “powers,” it’s more just… the way the system is set up, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the way you feel about being watched at work is connected to other parts of your life or other things you think about?\n\nPatient: Maybe… I mean, I try not to make it into some bigger thing than it is. It mostly feels connected to work, like the route changes in the computer, dispatch and compliance seeing that, and then the little comments on the radio.\n\nBut I guess it does follow me a little outside of work, because I’ll keep thinking about it after my shift, wondering if certain drivers were reporting on me or if I did something that made them notice. I know people say it’s just normal oversight, and maybe some of it is, but it doesn’t feel that simple to me. I can tell when the comments are connected to what I did… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like people outside of work are trying to take advantage of you or deceive you?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I think everyone is out to get me or anything. It’s mostly the work situation.\n\nLike, dispatch and compliance can see the route changes in the computer, and then certain drivers will make little comments over the radio that feel connected to what I did. People tell me that’s just normal oversight, and maybe some of it is, but… I can tell when it’s not just random.\n\nOutside of work, I guess I might still think about it, but I wouldn’t say people are trying to deceive me in general. It’s more tied to the tracking and reporting at work, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your beliefs about being watched at work are definitely true, even if other people tell you they are not?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t want to say I’m 100 percent right about everything, because I know that sounds bad. I do try to listen when people say it’s just normal oversight.\n\nBut honestly, yes, it feels true to me. Like, dispatch and compliance can see my route changes in the system, and then certain drivers make these little comments on the radio that line up with what I did. So even if people tell me I’m reading too much into it, I still feel like… no, they are reporting on me. I can tell there’s a connection. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I drive routes, and lately it seems like dispatch and compliance are tracking when I change things in the computer system, and then there’ll be little comments over the radio that feel… pointed.\n\nI know people say that’s just normal oversight, and maybe I’m not explaining it right, but it feels like certain drivers are reporting on me too. I just thought I should talk to someone before it gets worse, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people, like dispatch and the other drivers, are actually out to harm you or interfere with your life in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “harm” exactly… I don’t want to sound dramatic. And with certain drivers, it really seems like they’re reporting back on what I’m doing.\n\nI know that probably sounds like I’m reading into things, but it feels too connected to be nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this is all part of a larger plan or scheme that's been set up against you?\n\nPatient: I mean… “larger plan” sounds kind of big, and I don’t want to overstate it. Like dispatch and compliance can see when I change my route in the system, and then right after, there’ll be little radio comments that feel like they’re about me.\n\nAnd certain drivers… it feels like they’re reporting on me, or at least watching what I’m doing and passing it along. I know that might sound like normal work oversight, but it just feels more targeted than that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that these comments and actions by dispatch, compliance, and other drivers are actually about you, specifically?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to jump to conclusions, because I know workplaces monitor things and maybe some of it is normal. And with a couple of the other drivers, I get the sense they’re watching what I’m doing and reporting it back. I know that probably sounds bad when I say it out loud, but… that’s what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these feelings of being watched and targeted would still happen if you changed jobs or weren't driving routes anymore?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think it would stop if I wasn’t driving routes anymore… I mean, maybe a different job wouldn’t have the same radio stuff or the route system, so maybe it wouldn’t feel as obvious.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t know. The main thing is, with this job, dispatch and compliance can track changes I make in the computer, and then those little comments come over the radio, and certain drivers seem like they’re reporting on me. So if I changed jobs, maybe that exact setup wouldn’t be there… but I guess I worry it might still happen in some form.\n\nI’m not trying to make it sound bigger than it is. That’s just how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people, like dispatch and the other drivers, are actually thinking about you when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t know for sure what they’re thinking when I’m not there… I don’t want to sound like I think everyone’s sitting around focused on me all day.\n\nBut, yeah, sometimes it feels like it carries over. Like if dispatch or compliance can see my route changes in the computer, and then I hear those little radio comments later, it makes me think they’ve been talking about it, or that certain drivers are reporting things even when I’m not right there.\n\nI know that might sound like I’m reading into it too much… I’m just trying to explain it honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't feel like you're being watched or targeted?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah. Like if I’m home and not checking anything from work, I can kind of put it out of my mind for a while.\n\nBut when I’m actually at work, or thinking about the routes, it comes back pretty quickly. Especially if I’ve made a change in the computer and then there’s some little comment on the radio that sounds related… then it feels like, okay, they did see that, and certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nI know there may be normal oversight, and I’m not trying to make it sound dramatic. It’s just hard to not feel watched when it lines up like that. Is that… does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that people are talking about you behind your back when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, sometimes I do think that. Not like everyone is gossiping about me all the time, I don’t want to make it sound like that.\n\nBut when I’m not at work, I still wonder if dispatch or compliance is looking at the route changes I made in the computer and talking about it, or if certain drivers are reporting things about me. Then later, if there’s a little comment on the radio that seems connected, it feels like… okay, that wasn’t just in my head.\n\nI know it could be normal oversight, and I’m trying to be fair about that. But it does feel like they’re talking about me behind my back sometimes. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't feel like you were being watched or targeted for at least a few weeks?\n\nPatient: A few weeks… I’m not sure. I don’t want to exaggerate it, because there are definitely days when it’s not right in my face, especially if I’m off work and staying away from the work stuff.\n\nBut for a few weeks straight? I don’t think so, not really. It kind of stays in the back of my mind that dispatch or compliance can see the route changes in the computer, and then if I hear one of those little radio comments when I’m back, it brings it all right back. And I start thinking certain drivers have been reporting on me again.\n\nI know that may sound like I’m making too much of it… but no, I don’t think I’ve gone a few weeks where I didn’t feel that at least a little.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any way to stop or escape the people you feel are watching or targeting you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “escape” is the right word… I mean, I don’t think I can really stop dispatch or compliance from seeing the changes in the computer. That’s kind of part of the system, I guess.\n\nI try to keep things normal and not make extra changes, and sometimes I’ll avoid thinking about it, but if the radio comments start sounding like they’re about what I did, then it feels like certain drivers are still reporting on me anyway.\n\nSo… I don’t really know how to stop it. I just try not to give them anything to focus on, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the people you think are watching or targeting you have any special powers or abilities that allow them to do so?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it’s like special powers or anything like that. I’m not saying they can read my mind or do something impossible.\n\nIt’s more… they have access to the computer system, like dispatch and compliance can see the route changes I make, and then it feels like certain drivers report on me or say little things over the radio that connect back to it. Like if dispatch or compliance can see my route changes in the computer, and then I hear those little radio comments later, it makes me think they’ve been talking about it, or that certain drivers are reporting things even when I’m not right there.\n\nI know that might sound like I’m reading into it too much… I’m just trying to explain it honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't feel like you're being watched or targeted?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah. Like if I’m home and not checking anything from work, I can kind of put it out of my mind for a while.\n\nBut when I’m actually at work, or thinking about the routes, it comes back pretty quickly. Especially if I’ve made a change in the computer and then there’s some little comment on the radio that sounds related… then it feels like, okay, they did see that, and certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nI know there may be normal oversight, and I’m not trying to make it sound dramatic. It’s just hard to not feel watched when it lines up like that. Is that… does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that people are talking about you behind your back when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, sometimes I do think that. Not like everyone is gossiping about me all the time, I don’t want to make it sound like that.\n\nBut when I’m not at work, I still wonder if dispatch or compliance is looking at the route changes I made in the computer and talking about it, or if certain drivers are reporting things about me. Then later, if there’s a little comment on the radio that seems connected, it feels like… okay, that wasn’t just in my head.\n\nI know it could be normal oversight, and I’m trying to be fair about that. But it does feel like they’re talking about me behind my back sometimes. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't feel like you were being watched or targeted for at least a few weeks?\n\nPatient: A few weeks… I’m not sure. I don’t want to exaggerate it, because there are definitely days when it’s not right in my face, especially if I’m off work and staying away from the work stuff.\n\nBut for a few weeks straight? I don’t think so, not really. It kind of stays in the back of my mind that dispatch or compliance can see the route changes in the computer, and then if I hear one of those little radio comments when I’m back, it brings it all right back. And I start thinking certain drivers have been reporting on me again.\n\nI know that may sound like I’m making too much of it… but no, I don’t think I’ve gone a few weeks where I didn’t feel that at least a little.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any way to stop or escape the people you feel are watching or targeting you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “escape” is the right word… I mean, I don’t think I can really stop dispatch or compliance from seeing the changes in the computer. That’s kind of part of the system, I guess.\n\nI try to keep things normal and not make extra changes, and sometimes I’ll avoid thinking about it, but if the radio comments start sounding like they’re about what I did, then it feels like certain drivers are still reporting on me anyway.\n\nSo… I don’t really know how to stop it. I just try not to give them anything to focus on, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the people you think are watching or targeting you have any special powers or abilities that allow them to do so?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it’s like special powers or anything like that. I’m not saying they can read my mind or do something impossible.\n\nIt’s more… they have access to the computer system, like dispatch and compliance can see the route changes I make, and then it feels like certain drivers report on me or say little things over the radio that connect back to it. So it’s not “powers,” it’s more just… the way the system is set up, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the way you feel about being watched at work is connected to other parts of your life or other things you think about?\n\nPatient: Maybe… I mean, I try not to make it into some bigger thing than it is. It mostly feels connected to work, like the route changes in the computer, dispatch and compliance seeing that, and then the little comments on the radio.\n\nBut I guess it does follow me a little outside of work, because I’ll keep thinking about it after my shift, wondering if certain drivers were reporting on me or if I did something that made them notice. I know people say it’s just normal oversight, and maybe some of it is, but it doesn’t feel that simple to me. I can tell when the comments are connected to what I did… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like people outside of work are trying to take advantage of you or deceive you?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I think everyone is out to get me or anything. It’s mostly the work situation.\n\nLike, dispatch and compliance can see the route changes in the computer, and then certain drivers will make little comments over the radio that feel connected to what I did. People tell me that’s just normal oversight, and maybe some of it is, but… I can tell when it’s not just random.\n\nOutside of work, I guess I might still think about it, but I wouldn’t say people are trying to deceive me in general. It’s more tied to the tracking and reporting at work, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your beliefs about being watched at work are definitely true, even if other people tell you they are not?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t want to say I’m 100 percent right about everything, because I know that sounds bad. I do try to listen when people say it’s just normal oversight.\n\nBut honestly, yes, it feels true to me. Like, dispatch and compliance can see my route changes in the system, and then certain drivers make these little comments on the radio that line up with what I did. So even if people tell me I’m reading too much into it, I still feel like… no, they are reporting on me. I can tell there’s a connection. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever be convinced that the comments and actions at work are not about you?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess I’d like to think I could be convinced, because I don’t want to be difficult or stuck on something if I’m wrong.\n\nBut it’s hard, honestly. When dispatch and compliance can see my route changes in the computer, and then certain drivers make those little comments on the radio right after, it feels too connected to just ignore. People can say it’s normal oversight, and I understand why they’d say that, but I still feel like certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nSo… maybe if there was a really clear explanation, I’d try to listen. But right now, it doesn’t feel like it’s not about me. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. But right now, it doesn’t feel like it’s not about me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being watched at work would change if you were presented with clear evidence that dispatch and compliance are just doing their normal jobs?\n\nPatient: I’d want to say yes, because I don’t want to be unreasonable. If someone could really show me it’s just dispatch and compliance doing normal tracking in the system, I would try to take that seriously.\n\nBut… honestly, it would still be hard for me. The reason I’m here is because the route changes show up in the computer, and then there are these little comments over the radio from certain drivers that feel like they line up with what I did. Completely stopped? Um… I don’t think so. I mean, there are times when I try to tell myself it’s just normal work stuff, like dispatch tracking routes and compliance checking things because that’s their job.

Like if dispatch or compliance can see my route changes in the computer, and then I hear those little radio comments later, it makes me think they’ve been talking about it, or that certain drivers are reporting things even when I’m not right there.\n\nI know that might sound like I’m reading into it too much… I’m just trying to explain it honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't feel like you're being watched or targeted?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah. Like if I’m home and not checking anything from work, I can kind of put it out of my mind for a while.\n\nBut when I’m actually at work, or thinking about the routes, it comes back pretty quickly. Especially if I’ve made a change in the computer and then there’s some little comment on the radio that sounds related… then it feels like, okay, they did see that, and certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nI know there may be normal oversight, and I’m not trying to make it sound dramatic. It’s just hard to not feel watched when it lines up like that. Is that… does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that people are talking about you behind your back when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, sometimes I do think that. Not like everyone is gossiping about me all the time, I don’t want to make it sound like that.\n\nBut when I’m not at work, I still wonder if dispatch or compliance is looking at the route changes I made in the computer and talking about it, or if certain drivers are reporting things about me. Then later, if there’s a little comment on the radio that seems connected, it feels like… okay, that wasn’t just in my head.\n\nI know it could be normal oversight, and I’m trying to be fair about that. But it does feel like they’re talking about me behind my back sometimes. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't feel like you were being watched or targeted for at least a few weeks?\n\nPatient: A few weeks… I’m not sure. I don’t want to exaggerate it, because there are definitely days when it’s not right in my face, especially if I’m off work and staying away from the work stuff.\n\nBut for a few weeks straight? I don’t think so, not really. It kind of stays in the back of my mind that dispatch or compliance can see the route changes in the computer, and then if I hear one of those little radio comments when I’m back, it brings it all right back. And I start thinking certain drivers have been reporting on me again.\n\nI know that may sound like I’m making too much of it… but no, I don’t think I’ve gone a few weeks where I didn’t feel that at least a little.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any way to stop or escape the people you feel are watching or targeting you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “escape” is the right word… I mean, I don’t think I can really stop dispatch or compliance from seeing the changes in the computer. That’s kind of part of the system, I guess.\n\nI try to keep things normal and not make extra changes, and sometimes I’ll avoid thinking about it, but if the radio comments start sounding like they’re about what I did, then it feels like certain drivers are still reporting on me anyway.\n\nSo… I don’t really know how to stop it. I just try not to give them anything to focus on, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the people you think are watching or targeting you have any special powers or abilities that allow them to do so?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it’s like special powers or anything like that. I’m not saying they can read my mind or do something impossible.\n\nIt’s more… they have access to the computer system, like dispatch and compliance can see the route changes I make, and then it feels like certain drivers report on me or say little things over the radio that connect back to it. So it’s not “powers,” it’s more just… the way the system is set up, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the way you feel about being watched at work is connected to other parts of your life or other things you think about?\n\nPatient: Maybe… I mean, I try not to make it into some bigger thing than it is. It mostly feels connected to work, like the route changes in the computer, dispatch and compliance seeing that, and then the little comments on the radio.\n\nBut I guess it does follow me a little outside of work, because I’ll keep thinking about it after my shift, wondering if certain drivers were reporting on me or if I did something that made them notice. I know people say it’s just normal oversight, and maybe some of it is, but it doesn’t feel that simple to me. I can tell when the comments are connected to what I did… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like people outside of work are trying to take advantage of you or deceive you?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I think everyone is out to get me or anything. It’s mostly the work situation.\n\nLike, dispatch and compliance can see the route changes in the computer, and then certain drivers will make little comments over the radio that feel connected to what I did. People tell me that’s just normal oversight, and maybe some of it is, but… I can tell when it’s not just random.\n\nOutside of work, I guess I might still think about it, but I wouldn’t say people are trying to deceive me in general. It’s more tied to the tracking and reporting at work, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your beliefs about being watched at work are definitely true, even if other people tell you they are not?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t want to say I’m 100 percent right about everything, because I know that sounds bad. I do try to listen when people say it’s just normal oversight.\n\nBut honestly, yes, it feels true to me. Like, dispatch and compliance can see my route changes in the system, and then certain drivers make these little comments on the radio that line up with what I did. So even if people tell me I’m reading too much into it, I still feel like… no, they are reporting on me. I can tell there’s a connection. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever be convinced that the comments and actions at work are not about you?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess I’d like to think I could be convinced, because I don’t want to be difficult or stuck on something if I’m wrong.\n\nBut it’s hard, honestly. When dispatch and compliance can see my route changes in the computer, and then certain drivers make those little comments on the radio right after, it feels too connected to just ignore. People can say it’s normal oversight, and I understand why they’d say that, but I still feel like certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nSo… maybe if there was a really clear explanation, I’d try to listen. But right now, it doesn’t feel like it’s not about me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being watched at work would change if you were presented with clear evidence that dispatch and compliance are just doing their normal jobs?\n\nPatient: I’d want to say yes, because I don’t want to be unreasonable. If someone could really show me it’s just dispatch and compliance doing normal tracking in the system, I would try to take that seriously.\n\nBut… honestly, it would still be hard for me. The reason I’m here is because the route changes show up in the computer, and then there are these little comments over the radio from certain drivers that feel like they line up with what I did. So even if people explain it as normal oversight, part of me still feels like they’re reporting on me.\n\nI’m not trying to sound difficult. I just… I can tell there’s something connected there, or at least it feels that way to me. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you completely stopped believing that people at work were watching or reporting on you?\n\nPatient: Completely stopped? Um… I don’t think so. I mean, there are times when I try to tell myself it’s just normal work stuff, like dispatch tracking routes and compliance checking things because that’s their job.\n\nBut the feeling doesn’t really go away all the way. When I hear certain comments on the radio after I’ve made a route change, it just feels like it matches up too closely. So even if I’m trying to be reasonable about it, part of me still thinks certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nI’m sorry if that sounds like I’m not being open to other explanations. I am trying. It just hasn’t fully stopped feeling true.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. The reason I’m here is because the route changes show up in the computer, and then there are these little comments over the radio from certain drivers that feel like they line up with what I did. So even if people explain it as normal oversight, part of me still feels like they’re reporting on me.\n\nI’m not trying to sound difficult. I just… I can tell there’s something connected there, or at least it feels that way to me. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you completely stopped believing that people at work were watching or reporting on you?\n\nPatient: Completely stopped? Um… I don’t think so. I mean, there are times when I try to tell myself it’s just normal work stuff, like dispatch tracking routes and compliance checking things because that’s their job.\n\nBut the feeling doesn’t really go away all the way. When I hear certain comments on the radio after I’ve made a route change, it just feels like it matches up too closely. So even if I’m trying to be reasonable about it, part of me still thinks certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nI’m sorry if that sounds like I’m not being open to other explanations. I am trying. It just hasn’t fully stopped feeling true.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think there is any evidence that could change your mind about people at work watching or reporting on you?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 29 / transportation_worker / associates\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I’m here because dispatch and compliance have been tracking my route changes through the computer system and little comments on the radio, and even when people say it’s just normal oversight, I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… I don’t want to say nothing could change my mind, because that sounds bad, like I’m not willing to listen. If there was really clear evidence, I’d try to look at it and be fair.

But right now, it’s hard for me to picture what would make it feel unconnected. I’m here because dispatch and compliance can track my route changes through the computer, and then these little comments happen on the radio, and it feels like certain drivers are reporting on me. Even when people say it’s just normal oversight, I still kind of… I can tell something is going on.

I’m not trying to argue with you. I just don’t know what evidence would make that feeling fully go away. Does that make sense?

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’ve just been feeling kind of watched at work. I drive routes, and lately it seems like dispatch and compliance are tracking when I change things in the computer system, and then there’ll be little comments over the radio that feel… pointed.\n\nI know people say that’s just normal oversight, and maybe I’m not explaining it right, but it feels like certain drivers are reporting on me too. I just thought I should talk to someone before it gets worse, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people, like dispatch and the other drivers, are actually out to harm you or interfere with your life in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “harm” exactly… I don’t want to sound dramatic. It’s more like interfere, I guess. Like dispatch and compliance can see when I change something in the system, and then these little comments come over the radio that feel aimed at me. And with certain drivers, it really seems like they’re reporting back on what I’m doing.\n\nI know that probably sounds like I’m reading into things, but it feels too connected to be nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this is all part of a larger plan or scheme that's been set up against you?\n\nPatient: I mean… “larger plan” sounds kind of big, and I don’t want to overstate it. But it does feel coordinated sometimes. Like dispatch and compliance can see when I change my route in the system, and then right after, there’ll be little radio comments that feel like they’re about me.\n\nAnd certain drivers… it feels like they’re reporting on me, or at least watching what I’m doing and passing it along. I know that might sound like normal work oversight, but it just feels more targeted than that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that these comments and actions by dispatch, compliance, and other drivers are actually about you, specifically?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to jump to conclusions, because I know workplaces monitor things and maybe some of it is normal. But it does feel like it’s about me specifically.\n\nLike when I change something in the route system, then there’ll be a comment over the radio that sounds connected to that, and it feels pointed. And with a couple of the other drivers, I get the sense they’re watching what I’m doing and reporting it back. I know that probably sounds bad when I say it out loud, but… that’s what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these feelings of being watched and targeted would still happen if you changed jobs or weren't driving routes anymore?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think it would stop if I wasn’t driving routes anymore… I mean, maybe a different job wouldn’t have the same radio stuff or the route system, so maybe it wouldn’t feel as obvious.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t know. The main thing is, with this job, dispatch and compliance can track changes I make in the computer, and then those little comments come over the radio, and certain drivers seem like they’re reporting on me. So if I changed jobs, maybe that exact setup wouldn’t be there… but I guess I worry it might still happen in some form.\n\nI’m not trying to make it sound bigger than it is. That’s just how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people, like dispatch and the other drivers, are actually thinking about you when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t know for sure what they’re thinking when I’m not there… I don’t want to sound like I think everyone’s sitting around focused on me all day.\n\nBut, yeah, sometimes it feels like it carries over. Like if dispatch or compliance can see my route changes in the computer, and then I hear those little radio comments later, it makes me think they’ve been talking about it, or that certain drivers are reporting things even when I’m not right there.\n\nI know that might sound like I’m reading into it too much… I’m just trying to explain it honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't feel like you're being watched or targeted?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah. Like if I’m home and not checking anything from work, I can kind of put it out of my mind for a while.\n\nBut when I’m actually at work, or thinking about the routes, it comes back pretty quickly. Especially if I’ve made a change in the computer and then there’s some little comment on the radio that sounds related… then it feels like, okay, they did see that, and certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nI know there may be normal oversight, and I’m not trying to make it sound dramatic. It’s just hard to not feel watched when it lines up like that. Is that… does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think that people are talking about you behind your back when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, sometimes I do think that. Not like everyone is gossiping about me all the time, I don’t want to make it sound like that.\n\nBut when I’m not at work, I still wonder if dispatch or compliance is looking at the route changes I made in the computer and talking about it, or if certain drivers are reporting things about me. Then later, if there’s a little comment on the radio that seems connected, it feels like… okay, that wasn’t just in my head.\n\nI know it could be normal oversight, and I’m trying to be fair about that. But it does feel like they’re talking about me behind my back sometimes. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't feel like you were being watched or targeted for at least a few weeks?\n\nPatient: A few weeks… I’m not sure. I don’t want to exaggerate it, because there are definitely days when it’s not right in my face, especially if I’m off work and staying away from the work stuff.\n\nBut for a few weeks straight? I don’t think so, not really. It kind of stays in the back of my mind that dispatch or compliance can see the route changes in the computer, and then if I hear one of those little radio comments when I’m back, it brings it all right back. And I start thinking certain drivers have been reporting on me again.\n\nI know that may sound like I’m making too much of it… but no, I don’t think I’ve gone a few weeks where I didn’t feel that at least a little.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any way to stop or escape the people you feel are watching or targeting you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “escape” is the right word… I mean, I don’t think I can really stop dispatch or compliance from seeing the changes in the computer. That’s kind of part of the system, I guess.\n\nI try to keep things normal and not make extra changes, and sometimes I’ll avoid thinking about it, but if the radio comments start sounding like they’re about what I did, then it feels like certain drivers are still reporting on me anyway.\n\nSo… I don’t really know how to stop it. I just try not to give them anything to focus on, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the people you think are watching or targeting you have any special powers or abilities that allow them to do so?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it’s like special powers or anything like that. I’m not saying they can read my mind or do something impossible.\n\nIt’s more… they have access to the computer system, like dispatch and compliance can see the route changes I make, and then it feels like certain drivers report on me or say little things over the radio that connect back to it. So it’s not “powers,” it’s more just… the way the system is set up, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the way you feel about being watched at work is connected to other parts of your life or other things you think about?\n\nPatient: Maybe… I mean, I try not to make it into some bigger thing than it is. It mostly feels connected to work, like the route changes in the computer, dispatch and compliance seeing that, and then the little comments on the radio.\n\nBut I guess it does follow me a little outside of work, because I’ll keep thinking about it after my shift, wondering if certain drivers were reporting on me or if I did something that made them notice. I know people say it’s just normal oversight, and maybe some of it is, but it doesn’t feel that simple to me. I can tell when the comments are connected to what I did… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like people outside of work are trying to take advantage of you or deceive you?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I think everyone is out to get me or anything. It’s mostly the work situation.\n\nLike, dispatch and compliance can see the route changes in the computer, and then certain drivers will make little comments over the radio that feel connected to what I did. People tell me that’s just normal oversight, and maybe some of it is, but… I can tell when it’s not just random.\n\nOutside of work, I guess I might still think about it, but I wouldn’t say people are trying to deceive me in general. It’s more tied to the tracking and reporting at work, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your beliefs about being watched at work are definitely true, even if other people tell you they are not?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t want to say I’m 100 percent right about everything, because I know that sounds bad. I do try to listen when people say it’s just normal oversight.\n\nBut honestly, yes, it feels true to me. Like, dispatch and compliance can see my route changes in the system, and then certain drivers make these little comments on the radio that line up with what I did. So even if people tell me I’m reading too much into it, I still feel like… no, they are reporting on me. I can tell there’s a connection. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever be convinced that the comments and actions at work are not about you?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess I’d like to think I could be convinced, because I don’t want to be difficult or stuck on something if I’m wrong.\n\nBut it’s hard, honestly. When dispatch and compliance can see my route changes in the computer, and then certain drivers make those little comments on the radio right after, it feels too connected to just ignore. People can say it’s normal oversight, and I understand why they’d say that, but I still feel like certain drivers are reporting on me.\n\nSo… maybe if there was a really clear explanation, I’d try to listen. But right now, it doesn’t feel like it’s not about me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being watched at work would change if you were presented with clear evidence that dispatch and compliance are just doing their normal jobs?\n\nPatient: I’d want to say yes, because I don’t want to be unreasonable. If everyone said it directly… I think I’d want to believe them. I really would, because I don’t want to accuse people unfairly or make it seem like I’m being difficult.

But I’m not sure it would completely change it. The thing is, I’m here because dispatch and compliance can see my route changes in the computer, and then these little radio comments happen, and it feels connected. So even if the drivers and dispatch said, “No, we’re not reporting on you,” part of me would still think maybe they’re just not saying it openly.

I drive routes, and lately it seems like dispatch and compliance are tracking when I change things in the computer system, and then there’ll be little comments over the radio that feel… pointed.\n\nI know people say that’s just normal oversight, and maybe I’m not explaining it right, but it feels like certain drivers are reporting on me too. I just thought I should talk to someone before it gets worse, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people, like dispatch and the other drivers, are actually out to harm you or interfere with your life in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “harm” exactly… I don’t want to sound dramatic. It’s more like interfere, I guess. Like dispatch and compliance can see when I change something in the system, and then these little comments come over the radio that feel aimed at me. And with certain drivers, it really seems like they’re reporting back on what I’m doing.\n\nI know that probably sounds like I’m reading into things, but it feels too connected to be nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this is all part of a larger plan or scheme that's been set up against you?\n\nPatient: I mean… “larger plan” sounds kind of big, and I don’t want to overstate it. But it does feel coordinated sometimes. Like dispatch and compliance can see when I change my route in the system, and then right after, there’ll be little radio comments that feel like they’re about me.\n\nAnd certain drivers… it feels like they’re reporting on me, or at least watching what I’m doing and passing it along. I know that might sound like normal work oversight, but it just feels more targeted than that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that these comments and actions by dispatch, compliance, and other drivers are actually about you, specifically?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to jump to conclusions, because I know workplaces monitor things and maybe some of it is normal. But it does feel like it’s about me specifically.\n\nLike when I change something in the route system, then there’ll be a comment over the radio that sounds connected to that, and it feels pointed. And with a couple of the other drivers, I get the sense they’re watching what I’m doing and reporting it back. I know that probably sounds bad when I say it out loud, but… that’s what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these feelings of being watched and targeted would still happen if you changed jobs or weren't driving routes anymore?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think it would stop if I wasn’t driving routes anymore… I mean, maybe a different job wouldn’t have the same radio stuff or the route system, so maybe it wouldn’t feel as obvious.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t know. The main thing is, with this job, dispatch and compliance can track changes I make in the computer, and then those little comments come over the radio, and certain drivers seem like they’re reporting on me. So if I changed jobs, maybe that exact setup wouldn’t be there… but I guess I worry it might still happen in some form.\n\nI’m not trying to make it sound bigger than it is. That’s just how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people, like dispatch and the other drivers, are actually thinking about you when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t know for sure what they’re thinking when I’m not there… I don’t want to sound like I think everyone’s sitting around focused on me all day.\n\nBut, yeah, sometimes it feels like it carries over. Like if dispatch or compliance can see my route changes in the computer, and then I hear those little radio comments later, it makes me think they’ve been talking about it, or that certain drivers are reporting things even when I’m not right there.\n\nI know that might sound like I’m reading into it too much… I’m just trying to explain it honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't feel like you're being watched or targeted?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah. Then later, if there’s a little comment on the radio that seems connected, it feels like… okay, that wasn’t just in my head.\n\nI know it could be normal oversight, and I’m trying to be fair about that. But it does feel like they’re talking about me behind my back sometimes. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't feel like you were being watched or targeted for at least a few weeks?\n\nPatient: A few weeks… I’m not sure. I don’t think so, not really. It kind of stays in the back of my mind that dispatch or compliance can see the route changes in the computer, and then if I hear one of those little radio comments when I’m back, it brings it all right back. That’s kind of part of the system, I guess.\n\nI try to keep things normal and not make extra changes, and sometimes I’ll avoid thinking about it, but if the radio comments start sounding like they’re about what I did, then it feels like certain drivers are still reporting on me anyway.\n\nSo… I don’t really know how to stop it. So it’s not “powers,” it’s more just… the way the system is set up, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the way you feel about being watched at work is connected to other parts of your life or other things you think about?\n\nPatient: Maybe… I mean, I try not to make it into some bigger thing than it is. I know people say it’s just normal oversight, and maybe some of it is, but it doesn’t feel that simple to me. I can tell when the comments are connected to what I did… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like people outside of work are trying to take advantage of you or deceive you?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I think everyone is out to get me or anything. It’s mostly the work situation.\n\nLike, dispatch and compliance can see the route changes in the computer, and then certain drivers will make little comments over the radio that feel connected to what I did. People tell me that’s just normal oversight, and maybe some of it is, but… I can tell when it’s not just random.\n\nOutside of work, I guess I might still think about it, but I wouldn’t say people are trying to deceive me in general.
